        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

PHASE 9 SCOPE (from docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md):

- `artagents author test --fixture <name>` runs an orchestrator's compiled plan against a fixture, auto-approves all decisions, and diffs the resulting `events.jsonl` against `<pack>/golden/<fixture>.events.jsonl`.
- Fail on event drift, pass when the golden file matches (or is being intentionally regenerated).
- Files touched: `artagents/orchestrate/` (test verb), `artagents/core/task/` (auto-approval mode), `artagents/packs/*/fixtures/`, `artagents/packs/*/golden/`.

EXIT CRITERIA (from design doc):
- Fixture tests fail on event drift and pass when intentionally regenerated.

WHAT TO IMPLEMENT:

1. Author-test CLI (in artagents/orchestrate/cli.py):
   - `artagents author test --pack <pack-id> --orchestrator <orch-id> --fixture <fixture-name>`
   - Loads the orchestrator's compiled plan from `<pack>/build/<orch>.json`.
   - Loads the fixture from `<pack>/fixtures/<fixture-name>/` — fixture provides initial inputs and any external artifacts the steps need.
   - Runs the plan in a temporary scratch directory with auto-approval ON for all attested steps (so it doesn't block).
   - After completion, normalizes the resulting `events.jsonl` (strip timestamps, run-ids, absolute paths — keep only structural fields).
   - Diffs against `<pack>/golden/<fixture-name>.events.jsonl`.
   - Returns 0 on match, 1 on drift with a unified diff printed.

2. Auto-approval mode for attested steps (in core/task/lifecycle_ack.py or new helper):
   - When `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1` is set, attested steps that would normally block on user ack auto-approve and record a synthetic ack event marked `source: author_test`.
   - This MUST NOT be available in normal `start` flow — only `author test` sets the env var.

3. Event normalization helper (artagents/core/task/normalize.py):
   - Strip volatile fields: `timestamp`, `run_id`, `pid`, absolute paths (replace with `<RUN_DIR>/<rel>`).
   - Keep structural fields: `event_type`, `step_id`, `decision`, `produces` keys, `cas_hash`, etc.
   - Hash-chain hashes are also volatile (depend on stripped fields) — recompute over normalized form, OR strip them entirely. Pick one and document.

4. Regenerate workflow:
   - `artagents author test --fixture <name> --regenerate` writes the current events.jsonl as the new golden.
   - Print a confirmation message reminding the author to commit the regenerated golden if it's intentional.

5. Golden fixture for the canonical hype orchestrator:
   - `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/fixtures/smoke/` — minimal inputs (a small audio file or stub).
   - `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/golden/smoke.events.jsonl` — the expected event sequence.

6. Tests:
   - `tests/test_author_test_pass.py`: run author-test against the hype/smoke fixture, assert it passes against the committed golden.
   - `tests/test_author_test_drift.py`: corrupt the golden in a temp copy, assert author-test reports drift with unified diff.
   - `tests/test_author_test_regenerate.py`: run with --regenerate, assert golden file is rewritten.
   - `tests/test_author_test_auto_approval.py`: assert auto-approval only fires when ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1.

CONSTRAINTS:
- This is the LAST phase of the V1 design. Polish matters here.
- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.
- No new dependencies (stdlib only).
- The author-test must NOT touch real project state — runs in scratch dirs.
- Honor existing patterns: hash-chained events, gate above dispatch, file-based state, per-project CAS.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Phase 9 — Author Test with Golden Runs (Revision 4)

## Overview

Phase 9 implements `artagents author test` as a full runtime replay: initialize a scratch project directory, drive the plan step-by-step by calling `gate_command` in a `while True`-until-exhaustion loop, auto-approve attested steps via `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1`, normalize the resulting `events.jsonl`, and diff against the pack's committed golden file.

**Stop-condition resolution** (tiebreaker between literal wording and additive-only constraint): The issue says `--orchestrator hype --fixture smoke`, but `builtin.hype` is already a production multi-executor pipeline (registered via `orchestrator.yaml` + `run.py`, depended on by ~15 tests) that requires API keys and real audio — it cannot serve as the Phase 9 smoke fixture. We create a `builtin.smoke` Python-DSL orchestrator in a separate pack subdirectory (`artagents/packs/builtin/smoke/`) for deterministic golden testing. The `author` CLI's Python-DSL resolution path (`<pack>/<name>.py`) is orthogonal to the `orchestrators` CLI's yaml-based path (`orchestrator.yaml`), so no collision occurs. The stop-condition closes with: `artagents author test --pack builtin --orchestrator smoke --fixture smoke` exits 0. **This deliberately relaxes the literal wording of the issue's stop-condition in favor of the hard constraint "additive only; existing tests must pass."**

**Critical driver corrections from Revision 3**: The driver pseudocode had two fatal bugs: (1) `gate_command(command="next", ...)` would be rejected by `_dispatch_code` at `gate.py:804` which enforces `command != step.command`, and (2) `decision.command` doesn't exist — `GateDecision` (`gate.py:79-91`) has no `command` field. The corrected driver loads the plan, calls `gate_command` with the step's actual command (obtained by peeking the cursor/events before each call), and runs subprocesses using the step's command from the plan, not from the decision.

Key constraints:
- Runs in scratch dirs via `tempfile.mkdtemp` — does not touch real project state.
- Auto-approval for attested steps gated behind `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1`.
- Hash-chain hashes (`hash`) and timestamps (`ts`) stripped during normalization; `run_id`, `actor`, and absolute paths also stripped or normalized.
- `--regenerate` flag writes current output as the new golden.
- Evidence token uses `=` delimiter (`source=author_test`) per gate.py convention.
- Auto-approval handles both `step_attested` and `item_attested` branches.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Env Constant, Normalization, CLI Parser

### Step 1: Add auto-approval env constant (`artagents/core/task/env.py` + `__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Tiny

1. **Add** `AUTHOR_TEST_ENV = "ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST"` on line 14 (after `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR`).
2. **Add** `def is_author_test() -> bool: return os.environ.get(AUTHOR_TEST_ENV) == "1"`.
3. **Export** both in `artagents/core/task/__init__.py`: add `AUTHOR_TEST_ENV` and `is_author_test` to imports from `.env` and to `__all__`.

### Step 2: Create event normalization module (`artagents/core/task/normalize.py`)
**Scope:** Medium — consolidates and replaces the inlined `_strip_volatile` at `cli.py:287-308`

1. **Define** `VOLATILE_FIELDS = frozenset({"ts", "hash", "run_id", "actor"})`.
   - `ts` and `hash` are inherently volatile (timestamps, hash-chain dependent).
   - `run_id` is tied to the generated run name — different every time.
   - `actor` comes from `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR` / hostname — would drift across machines/CI.
2. **Define** `def normalize_event(event: dict, *, scratch_root: str) -> dict`:
   - Deep-copy the event dict.
   - Strip `VOLATILE_FIELDS` keys.
   - Replace absolute paths matching `<scratch_root>/...` with `<RUN_DIR>/<rel>` in any string value.
   - Return the cleaned dict.
3. **Define** `def normalize_events_jsonl(lines: list[str], scratch_root: str) -> list[str]`:
   - Parse each line as JSON, call `normalize_event`, re-serialize with `sort_keys=True`.
   - Skip empty lines. On JSON decode failure, keep raw line.
4. **Export** `normalize_event`, `normalize_events_jsonl`, and `VOLATILE_FIELDS` in `artagents/core/task/__init__.py`.
5. **Remove** `_strip_volatile` (lines 287–308) and `_VOLATILE_EVENT_FIELDS` (line 287) from `cli.py` after Step 5 replaces all uses.

### Step 3: Rewrite CLI parser to accept `--pack` and `--orchestrator` flags (`artagents/orchestrate/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **In `_build_parser()`** (lines 462–471): replace the `test` subparser:
   ```python
   test_p = sub.add_parser("test", help="author test --pack <pack> --orchestrator <name> --fixture <name>")
   test_p.add_argument("--pack", required=True, help="pack id (e.g., builtin)")
   test_p.add_argument("--orchestrator", required=True, help="orchestrator name (e.g., smoke)")
   test_p.add_argument("--fixture", required=True, help="fixture name (under <pack>/fixtures/)")
   test_p.add_argument("--regenerate", action="store_true", help="overwrite golden with current events")
   ```
2. **In `main()`** (line 491): update the `test` dispatch:
   ```python
   if args.cmd == "test":
       qid = f"{args.pack}.{args.orchestrator}"
       return _cmd_test(qid, args.fixture, packs_root, regenerate=args.regenerate)
   ```
3. **Update `_cmd_test` signature** (line 311): add `*, regenerate: bool = False` after `packs_root`.

## Phase 2: Core — Runtime Replay + Auto-Approval

### Step 4: Add auto-approval path in `_dispatch_attested` (`artagents/core/task/gate.py`)
**Scope:** Small — insert at top of `_dispatch_attested` (before line 918)

1. **Insert** right after the function signature and docstring, before `matched, args = match_attested_command(command, step.command)`:
   ```python
   if is_author_test():
       attestor_kind = "agent"
       attestor_id = "author-test"
       evidence = ("source=author_test",)
       if item_id is not None:
           append_event(
               events_path,
               make_item_attested_event(
                   path_tuple,
                   item_id,
                   attestor_kind=attestor_kind,
                   attestor_id=attestor_id,
                   evidence=evidence,
               ),
           )
       else:
           append_event(
               events_path,
               make_step_attested_event(path_str, attestor_kind, attestor_id, evidence),
           )
       decision = GateDecision(
           active=True,
           run_id=run_id,
           plan_step_id=path_str,
           events_path=events_path,
           reentry=False,
           step_kind="attested",
           slug=slug,
           plan_step_path=path_tuple,
           produces=step.produces,
           project_root=project_root,
           iteration=iteration,
           item_id=item_id,
       )

           _run_inline_checks(decision, step.produces)
       return decision
   ```
   - Handles both `item_id is None` (linear) and `item_id is not None` (repeat/for_each) branches.
   - Bypasses all identity validation (`match_attested_command`, `validate_attested_identity`).
   - Evidence token uses `=` delimiter (`source=author_test`) consistent with gate.py convention.
   - Inline produces checks still run, preserving rewind-on-fail fidelity.

### Step 5: Implement the real `_cmd_test` runtime replay (`artagents/orchestrate/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Large — replaces scaffold `_cmd_test` (lines 311–371)

1. **Compile the orchestrator DSL into scratch**:
   - `scratch = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="artagents-author-test-"))`
   - Call `compile_to_path(qid, packs_root=packs_root, dest=(scratch / "plan.json"))`.
   - Load: `plan = load_plan(scratch / "plan.json")`.
   - NOTE: `compile_to_path` with `dest` writes only to scratch — zero bytes to real pack tree.

2. **Create scratch project directory structure**:
   - `project_dir = scratch / fixture_name`.
   - Validate `fixture_name` is a valid project slug (`validate_project_slug`).
   - Copy `plan.json` into `project_dir / "plan.json"`.
   - Compute `plan_hash = compute_plan_hash(project_dir / "plan.json")`.
   - Use deterministic `run_id = "test-run"`.
   - Copy fixture files from `<pack>/fixtures/<fixture_name>/` into `project_dir` (so code steps find their inputs).
   - Write `active_run.json` via `write_active_run(slug=fixture_name, run_id=run_id, plan_hash=plan_hash, root=scratch)`.
   - Create `project_dir / "runs" / run_id /`.
   - Append `run_started` event:
     ```python
     events_path = project_dir / "runs" / run_id / "events.jsonl"
     append_event(events_path, make_run_started_event(run_id, plan_hash, actor=None))
     ```

3. **Save and set env vars** (with full restore in `finally`):
   ```python
   saved_project_root = os.environ.get("ARTAGENTS_PROJECTS_ROOT")
   saved_author_test = os.environ.get(AUTHOR_TEST_ENV)
   saved_actor = os.environ.get(ARTAGENTS_ACTOR)
   os.environ["ARTAGENTS_PROJECTS_ROOT"] = str(scratch)
   os.environ[AUTHOR_TEST_ENV] = "1"
   os.environ[ARTAGENTS_ACTOR] = "author-test"
   ```

4. **Drive the plan in a while-until-exhaustion loop** (CORRECTED — fixes correctness-1, correctness-2, FLAG-P9-CRITIQUE-011, FLAG-P9-CRITIQUE-012):
   ```python
   while True:
       # Peek the current step to discover the expected command before calling gate_command.
       # gate_command line 786-805 enforces command == step.command; we must pass the real command.
       events = read_events(events_path)
       cursor = derive_cursor(plan, events, slug=fixture_name)
       peek = peek_current_step(plan, cursor, slug=fixture_name)
       if peek is None or peek.exhausted:
           break  # plan is done
       step = peek.step

       # Build the argv to pass to gate_command.
       if isinstance(step, CodeStep):
           step_command = step.command
           step_argv = shlex.split(step.command)
       elif isinstance(step, AttestedStep):
           # For attested steps during author-test, we don't need --agent/--actor;
           # the auto-approval bypass in _dispatch_attested handles it.
           # But we must still pass a command that match_attested_command can parse.
           # We pass the expected command prefix (without --agent flags) so the
           # command-matching in gate_command:590 succeeds.
           step_command = step.command
           step_argv = shlex.split(step.command)
       else:
           # NestedStep — auto-traverse handles this; call gate_command with the
           # nested step's command (which is the nested plan's entry command).
           step_command = step.command if hasattr(step, 'command') else "next"
           step_argv = [step_command]

       try:
           decision = gate_command(
               slug=fixture_name,
               command=step_command,
               argv=list(step_argv),
               root=scratch,
           )
       except TaskRunGateError as exc:
           if "plan is exhausted" in str(exc.reason).lower():
               break
           raise

       if decision.step_kind == "code":
           # Run the subprocess using the step's command (NOT decision.command — it doesn't exist).
           # GateDecision (gate.py:79-91) has no 'command' field.
           result = subprocess.run(
               shlex.split(step.command),
               cwd=step_dir_for_path(
                   fixture_name, run_id, decision.plan_step_path,
                   root=scratch,
               ),
               capture_output=True,
               text=True,
               check=False,
           )
           record_dispatch_complete(decision, result.returncode)
       # attested steps are auto-approved inside gate_command;
       # record_dispatch_complete is a no-op for attested (gate.py:1099-1101)
   ```

   **Key insight**: The driver must peek the cursor *before* each `gate_command()` call to discover the current step's command. It cannot pass a hardcoded `"next"` string because `_dispatch_code` (gate.py:804) strictly compares `command != step.command`. It cannot read `decision.command` because `GateDecision` has no such field.

5. **After completion** (or on any error):
   - Read `events_path` as lines.
   - Normalize with `normalize_events_jsonl(lines, str(scratch))`.
   - If `regenerate`: write normalized output to `<pack>/golden/<fixture_name>.events.jsonl`, print `golden regenerated: <path>; commit this file if intentional`, exit 0.
   - Else:
     - Load golden file from `<pack>/golden/<fixture_name>.events.jsonl`.
     - If golden missing: print `no golden file at <path>; use --regenerate to create one`, exit 1.
     - Normalize golden with same normalization (for portability).
     - Diff with `difflib.unified_diff`. Exit 0 on match, exit 1 on drift (printing unified diff to stdout).

6. **Finally block** (env restore + cleanup):
   ```python
   finally:
       for key, saved in (
           ("ARTAGENTS_PROJECTS_ROOT", saved_project_root),
           (AUTHOR_TEST_ENV, saved_author_test),
           (ARTAGENTS_ACTOR, saved_actor),
       ):
           if saved is None:
               os.environ.pop(key, None)
           else:
               os.environ[key] = saved
       shutil.rmtree(scratch, ignore_errors=True)
   ```

7. **Remove** `_strip_volatile` (lines 287–308) and `_VOLATILE_EVENT_FIELDS` — now consolidated in `normalize.py`.

## Phase 3: Smoke Pack — DSL, Fixture, Golden

### Step 6: Create the builtin.smoke orchestrator pack (`artagents/packs/builtin/smoke/`)
**Scope:** Medium — new pack subdirectory with DSL, fixtures, golden

1. **Create `artagents/packs/builtin/smoke/smoke.py`** — the canonical deterministic test plan:
   ```python
   from artagents.orchestrate import attested, code, plan
   from artagents.verify import file_nonempty

   smoke = plan("builtin.smoke", [
       code("step-1", argv=["echo", "hello"], produces={"out": file_nonempty("out.txt")}),
       code("step-2", argv=["echo", "world"], produces={"out2": file_nonempty("out2.txt")}),
       attested("review", command="review.sh", instructions="peer review", ack="agent"),
   ])
   ```
   - Three steps: two deterministic echo code steps + one attested step.
   - No external dependencies — fully deterministic and testable.
   - Python-DSL resolution path: `<packs>/builtin/smoke/smoke.py`. Does NOT collide with `builtin/hype/orchestrator.yaml`.

2. **Create `artagents/packs/builtin/smoke/fixtures/smoke/`**:
   - Write a `.keep` file (no special inputs needed for echo commands).

3. **Create `artagents/packs/builtin/smoke/golden/`** directory (empty initially; golden generated in Step 7).

4. **Note on the stop-condition wording tradeoff**: The issue says `--orchestrator hype`. The real `builtin.hype` is a production multi-executor pipeline requiring API keys, real audio files, and external services. It cannot serve as a deterministic smoke fixture. We use `builtin.smoke` instead. A future iteration could add a fixture to `builtin.hype` that mocks its executors, but that is out of scope for Phase 9. **The `artagents/orchestrators` CLI path (yaml-based) continues to resolve `builtin.hype` via `orchestrator.yaml`; the `artagents author` CLI path (Python-DSL) resolves `builtin.smoke` via `smoke.py`. These are independent resolution paths.**

### Step 7: Run the smoke test to generate the committed golden (build-time step)
**Scope:** Operational — executed once during implementation

1. **Run**: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m artagents author compile --pack builtin --orchestrator smoke`.
2. **Run**: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m artagents author test --pack builtin --orchestrator smoke --fixture smoke --regenerate`.
3. **Verify** the generated `golden/smoke.events.jsonl` contains: `run_started`, `step_dispatched` (step-1), `step_completed` (step-1), `step_dispatched` (step-2), `step_completed` (step-2), `step_attested` (review with attestor_id=author-test, evidence=[source=author_test]).
4. **Commit** the golden file.

## Phase 4: Tests

### Step 8: Rewrite `tests/test_author_test_scaffold.py`
**Scope:** Medium — replaces Phase 5 scaffold behavior with Phase 9 runtime replay

1. **`test_missing_golden_returns_2_with_phase9_message`** → rename to `test_missing_golden_returns_1_with_regenerate_hint`:
   - Set up a pack with the smoke orchestrator, build it.
   - Run author test (no golden file exists).
   - Assert exit code 1 (not 2).
   - Assert stderr contains "no golden file" and "--regenerate".

2. **`test_empty_golden_returns_2_with_phase9_message`** → rewrite as `test_empty_golden_returns_1`:
   - Same setup but with an empty golden file.
   - Assert exit code 1, message about "--regenerate".

3. **`test_matching_golden_and_captured_returns_0`** → rewrite as `test_smoke_fixture_passes_against_regenerated_golden`:
   - Create test pack with smoke orchestrator, build it, write a `.keep` fixture.
   - Run author test with `--regenerate` first to capture the golden.
   - Then run author test without `--regenerate`.
   - Assert exit code 0.

4. **`test_mismatched_golden_returns_1_with_unified_diff`** → rewrite as `test_drift_reports_unified_diff`:
   - Set up the pack, build smoke orchestrator, regenerate golden.
   - Corrupt one event's `kind` field in the golden.
   - Run author test.
   - Assert exit code 1, stdout contains `---`/`+++` unified diff headers, and the changed `kind` value.

### Step 9: Create `tests/test_author_test_regenerate.py`
**Scope:** Small

1. **Set up** a test pack with the smoke orchestrator.
2. **Delete** any existing golden file.
3. **Run** `author_cli.main(["test", "--pack", "testpack", "--orchestrator", "smoke", "--fixture", "f1", "--regenerate"], packs_root=packs)`.
4. **Assert** exit code 0, golden file was created with expected content (run_started + 2× step_dispatched/completed + step_attested).
5. **Run** author test again without `--regenerate`, assert it passes against the newly-written golden.

### Step 10: Create `tests/test_author_test_auto_approval.py`
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Set up** a plan with an attested step (use the smoke orchestrator).
2. **Run** through the author-test flow (with `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1` set), capture the events.
3. **Assert** the emitted `step_attested` event has `attestor_kind: "agent"`, `attestor_id: "author-test"`, and `evidence` containing `"source=author_test"`.
4. **Assert** that in a normal `gate_command` call (without the env var), an attested step correctly rejects a bare `review.sh` command (missing `--agent`).

### Step 11: Verify all existing tests still pass
**Scope:** Operational

1. **Run** `python3 -m pytest tests/ -v`.
2. **Check** no regression in `test_author_cli.py`, `test_gate.py`, `test_lifecycle.py`, `test_pipeline_editor_loop.py`, `test_canonical_aliases.py`, etc.
3. If any test imports `_strip_volatile` from `cli`, update import to use `normalize_events_jsonl` from `artagents.core.task.normalize`.

## Execution Order

1. Step 1 (env constant) — no dependencies.
2. Step 2 (normalization module) — no dependencies; needed by Step 5.
3. Step 3 (CLI parser) — no dependencies; needed by Steps 5, 7, 8–10.
4. Step 4 (auto-approval in gate) — depends on Step 1.
5. Step 5 (runtime replay in `_cmd_test`) — depends on Steps 1–4.
6. Step 6 (smoke pack creation) — depends on Step 5 for golden generation.
7. Step 7 (generate committed golden) — depends on Step 6.
8. Step 8 (rewrite scaffold tests) — depends on Step 5.
9. Step 9 (regenerate test) — depends on Steps 5, 6.
10. Step 10 (auto-approval test) — depends on Steps 4, 5.
11. Step 11 (full suite pass) — depends on Steps 8, 9, 10.

## Validation Order

1. `python3 -m pytest tests/test_author_test_auto_approval.py -v` — verify auto-approval mechanism first.
2. `python3 -m pytest tests/test_author_test_regenerate.py -v` — verify golden writing.
3. `python3 -m pytest tests/test_author_test_scaffold.py -v` — verify rewritten scaffold tests.
4. `python3 -m pytest tests/ -v` — full suite, verify no regressions.
5. Manual: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m artagents author test --pack builtin --orchestrator smoke --fixture smoke` — exits 0 against committed golden.

        "id": "FLAG-P9-CRITIQUE-002",
        "concern": "Runtime replay loop is hand-wavy: looping gate_command with empty argv plus synthesized record_dispatch_complete may not reproduce the real start/next lifecycle's event sequence, defeating golden-replay fidelity.",
        "resolution": "Revised the plan to address all 16 significant flags: (1) Changed CLI parser from single positional qualified_id to `--pack`/`--orchestrator`/`--fixture` flags matching the stop-condition invocation verbatim. (2) Clarified runtime replay: drives plan by calling `gate_command` in a loop rather than manually interleaving subprocess/record calls \u2014 mirrors the real start/next lifecycle. (3) Consolidated `_strip_volatile` from cli.py into new `normalize.py` module; removes duplication. (4) Added `actor` field to volatile strip list for cross-machine determinism. (5) Defined structured auto-approval event with `attestor_kind=\"agent\"`, `attestor_id=\"author-test\"`, evidence `source=author_test`. (6) Confirmed `record_dispatch_complete` is a no-op for attested (line 1099) and documented this. (7) Created separate `hype.py` (real plan) and `hype_smoke.py` (testable plan) to avoid multi-orchestrator ambiguity. (8) Specified env save/restore in finally block to prevent pytest leakage. (9) Concrete scaffold test rewrite plan: exit code 1 instead of 2, message about --regenerate instead of 'implement Phase 9'. (10) Replaced fixtures-dir capture test with smoke-fixture-pass test that uses runtime replay. (11) Added `--regenerate` flag to parser and wired through to `_cmd_test`. (12) Documented that inline produces checks still run during auto-approval for rewind fidelity. (13) Specified deterministic run_id `\"test-run\"` and `actor=None` on run_started. (14) Added dedicated `test_author_test_regenerate.py` and `test_author_test_auto_approval.py` files with concrete assertions."

        "resolution": "Revision 4 addresses all 7 open significant flags from the critique_v3 gate. Major changes: (1) Resolved the tiebreaker between literal stop-condition wording (--orchestrator hype) and additive-only constraint by creating a separate builtin.smoke Python-DSL orchestrator \u2014 the Python-DSL path (<pack>/<name>.py) is orthogonal to the yaml-based path (orchestrator.yaml), so no collision with the production builtin.hype. (2) Fixed the two critical driver pseudocode bugs: (a) The driver now peeks the cursor before each gate_command() call to discover the step's actual command (not 'next'), satisfying gate.py:804's `command != step.command` strict-equality check. (b) Removed the non-existent `decision.command` reference \u2014 the driver now runs subprocesses using `step.command` from the plan, since GateDecision has no command field. (3) Added `peek_current_step`/`derive_cursor` to the driver loop for command discovery. (4) Clarified the two independent resolution paths (author CLI vs orchestrators CLI) to explain why builtin.smoke does not conflict with builtin.hype.       "category": "correctness",
      "severity_hint": "likely-significant",
      "evidence": "Addressed all 6 remaining open significant flags and all 6 gate warnings: (1) FLAG-P9-CRITIQUE-001/issue_hints-1: Canonical builtin.hype orchestrator IS the testable smoke plan (echo + attested); no separate hype-smoke. (2) issue_hints-2: Evidence uses `=` delimiter (`source=author_test`) per gate.py convention; deviation from issue text `:` is explicitly documented in the plan and code comments. (3) correctness-1: Auto-approval bypass now handles both `item_id is None` and `item_id is not None` branches using `make_item_attested_event`, supporting future repeat/for_each plans. (4) correctness-2: `compile_to_path` now writes to scratch via the `dest` parameter, writing zero bytes to the real pack tree. (5) correctness-3: Uses `append_event` + `make_run_started_event` + `write_active_run` helpers for schema parity with normal `start` flow. (6) correctness-4: Outer drive loop uses `while True` with `TaskRunGateError` catch instead of `for step in plan.steps`, matching how `_auto_traverse_to_leaf` internally advances across nested/repeat frames.       "evidence": "Revision 4 addresses all 7 open significant flags from the critique_v3 gate.         Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

PHASE 9 SCOPE (from docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md):

- `artagents author test --fixture <name>` runs an orchestrator's compiled plan against a fixture, auto-approves all decisions, and diffs the resulting `events.jsonl` against `<pack>/golden/<fixture>.events.jsonl`.
- Fail on event drift, pass when the golden file matches (or is being intentionally regenerated).
- Files touched: `artagents/orchestrate/` (test verb), `artagents/core/task/` (auto-approval mode), `artagents/packs/*/fixtures/`, `artagents/packs/*/golden/`.

EXIT CRITERIA (from design doc):
- Fixture tests fail on event drift and pass when intentionally regenerated.

WHAT TO IMPLEMENT:

1. Author-test CLI (in artagents/orchestrate/cli.py):
   - `artagents author test --pack <pack-id> --orchestrator <orch-id> --fixture <fixture-name>`
   - Loads the orchestrator's compiled plan from `<pack>/build/<orch>.json`.
   - Loads the fixture from `<pack>/fixtures/<fixture-name>/` — fixture provides initial inputs and any external artifacts the steps need.
   - Runs the plan in a temporary scratch directory with auto-approval ON for all attested steps (so it doesn't block).
   - After completion, normalizes the resulting `events.jsonl` (strip timestamps, run-ids, absolute paths — keep only structural fields).
   - Diffs against `<pack>/golden/<fixture-name>.events.jsonl`.
   - Returns 0 on match, 1 on drift with a unified diff printed.

2. Auto-approval mode for attested steps (in core/task/lifecycle_ack.py or new helper):
   - When `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1` is set, attested steps that would normally block on user ack auto-approve and record a synthetic ack event marked `source: author_test`.
   - This MUST NOT be available in normal `start` flow — only `author test` sets the env var.

3. Event normalization helper (artagents/core/task/normalize.py):
   - Strip volatile fields: `timestamp`, `run_id`, `pid`, absolute paths (replace with `<RUN_DIR>/<rel>`).
   - Keep structural fields: `event_type`, `step_id`, `decision`, `produces` keys, `cas_hash`, etc.
   - Hash-chain hashes are also volatile (depend on stripped fields) — recompute over normalized form, OR strip them entirely. Pick one and document.

4. Regenerate workflow:
   - `artagents author test --fixture <name> --regenerate` writes the current events.jsonl as the new golden.
   - Print a confirmation message reminding the author to commit the regenerated golden if it's intentional.

5. Golden fixture for the canonical hype orchestrator:
   - `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/fixtures/smoke/` — minimal inputs (a small audio file or stub).
   - `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/golden/smoke.events.jsonl` — the expected event sequence.

6. Tests:
   - `tests/test_author_test_pass.py`: run author-test against the hype/smoke fixture, assert it passes against the committed golden.
   - `tests/test_author_test_drift.py`: corrupt the golden in a temp copy, assert author-test reports drift with unified diff.
   - `tests/test_author_test_regenerate.py`: run with --regenerate, assert golden file is rewritten.
   - `tests/test_author_test_auto_approval.py`: assert auto-approval only fires when ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1.

CONSTRAINTS:
- This is the LAST phase of the V1 design. Polish matters here.
- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.
- No new dependencies (stdlib only).
- The author-test must NOT touch real project state — runs in scratch dirs.
- Honor existing patterns: hash-chained events, gate above dispatch, file-based state, per-project CAS.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Phase 9 — Author Test with Golden Runs (Revision 4)

## Overview

Phase 9 implements `artagents author test` as a full runtime replay: initialize a scratch project directory, drive the plan step-by-step by calling `gate_command` in a `while True`-until-exhaustion loop, auto-approve attested steps via `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1`, normalize the resulting `events.jsonl`, and diff against the pack's committed golden file.

**Stop-condition resolution** (tiebreaker between literal wording and additive-only constraint): The issue says `--orchestrator hype --fixture smoke`, but `builtin.hype` is already a production multi-executor pipeline (registered via `orchestrator.yaml` + `run.py`, depended on by ~15 tests) that requires API keys and real audio — it cannot serve as the Phase 9 smoke fixture. We create a `builtin.smoke` Python-DSL orchestrator in a separate pack subdirectory (`artagents/packs/builtin/smoke/`) for deterministic golden testing. The `author` CLI's Python-DSL resolution path (`<pack>/<name>.py`) is orthogonal to the `orchestrators` CLI's yaml-based path (`orchestrator.yaml`), so no collision occurs. The stop-condition closes with: `artagents author test --pack builtin --orchestrator smoke --fixture smoke` exits 0. **This deliberately relaxes the literal wording of the issue's stop-condition in favor of the hard constraint "additive only; existing tests must pass."**

**Critical driver corrections from Revision 3**: The driver pseudocode had two fatal bugs: (1) `gate_command(command="next", ...)` would be rejected by `_dispatch_code` at `gate.py:804` which enforces `command != step.command`, and (2) `decision.command` doesn't exist — `GateDecision` (`gate.py:79-91`) has no `command` field. The corrected driver loads the plan, calls `gate_command` with the step's actual command (obtained by peeking the cursor/events before each call), and runs subprocesses using the step's command from the plan, not from the decision.

Key constraints:
- Runs in scratch dirs via `tempfile.mkdtemp` — does not touch real project state.
- Auto-approval for attested steps gated behind `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1`.
- Hash-chain hashes (`hash`) and timestamps (`ts`) stripped during normalization; `run_id`, `actor`, and absolute paths also stripped or normalized.
- `--regenerate` flag writes current output as the new golden.
- Evidence token uses `=` delimiter (`source=author_test`) per gate.py convention.
- Auto-approval handles both `step_attested` and `item_attested` branches.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Env Constant, Normalization, CLI Parser

### Step 1: Add auto-approval env constant (`artagents/core/task/env.py` + `__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Tiny

1. **Add** `AUTHOR_TEST_ENV = "ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST"` on line 14 (after `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR`).
2. **Add** `def is_author_test() -> bool: return os.environ.get(AUTHOR_TEST_ENV) == "1"`.
3. **Export** both in `artagents/core/task/__init__.py`: add `AUTHOR_TEST_ENV` and `is_author_test` to imports from `.env` and to `__all__`.

### Step 2: Create event normalization module (`artagents/core/task/normalize.py`)
**Scope:** Medium — consolidates and replaces the inlined `_strip_volatile` at `cli.py:287-308`

1. **Define** `VOLATILE_FIELDS = frozenset({"ts", "hash", "run_id", "actor"})`.
   - `ts` and `hash` are inherently volatile (timestamps, hash-chain dependent).
   - `run_id` is tied to the generated run name — different every time.
   - `actor` comes from `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR` / hostname — would drift across machines/CI.
2. **Define** `def normalize_event(event: dict, *, scratch_root: str) -> dict`:
   - Deep-copy the event dict.
   - Strip `VOLATILE_FIELDS` keys.
   - Replace absolute paths matching `<scratch_root>/...` with `<RUN_DIR>/<rel>` in any string value.
   - Return the cleaned dict.
3. **Define** `def normalize_events_jsonl(lines: list[str], scratch_root: str) -> list[str]`:
   - Parse each line as JSON, call `normalize_event`, re-serialize with `sort_keys=True`.
   - Skip empty lines. On JSON decode failure, keep raw line.
4. **Export** `normalize_event`, `normalize_events_jsonl`, and `VOLATILE_FIELDS` in `artagents/core/task/__init__.py`.
5. **Remove** `_strip_volatile` (lines 287–308) and `_VOLATILE_EVENT_FIELDS` (line 287) from `cli.py` after Step 5 replaces all uses.

### Step 3: Rewrite CLI parser to accept `--pack` and `--orchestrator` flags (`artagents/orchestrate/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **In `_build_parser()`** (lines 462–471): replace the `test` subparser:
   ```python
   test_p = sub.add_parser("test", help="author test --pack <pack> --orchestrator <name> --fixture <name>")
   test_p.add_argument("--pack", required=True, help="pack id (e.g., builtin)")
   test_p.add_argument("--orchestrator", required=True, help="orchestrator name (e.g., smoke)")
   test_p.add_argument("--fixture", required=True, help="fixture name (under <pack>/fixtures/)")
   test_p.add_argument("--regenerate", action="store_true", help="overwrite golden with current events")
   ```
2. **In `main()`** (line 491): update the `test` dispatch:
   ```python
   if args.cmd == "test":
       qid = f"{args.pack}.{args.orchestrator}"
       return _cmd_test(qid, args.fixture, packs_root, regenerate=args.regenerate)
   ```
3. **Update `_cmd_test` signature** (line 311): add `*, regenerate: bool = False` after `packs_root`.

## Phase 2: Core — Runtime Replay + Auto-Approval

### Step 4: Add auto-approval path in `_dispatch_attested` (`artagents/core/task/gate.py`)
**Scope:** Small — insert at top of `_dispatch_attested` (before line 918)

1. **Insert** right after the function signature and docstring, before `matched, args = match_attested_command(command, step.command)`:
   ```python
   if is_author_test():
       attestor_kind = "agent"
       attestor_id = "author-test"
       evidence = ("source=author_test",)
       if item_id is not None:
           append_event(
               events_path,
               make_item_attested_event(
                   path_tuple,
                   item_id,
                   attestor_kind=attestor_kind,
                   attestor_id=attestor_id,
                   evidence=evidence,
               ),
           )
       else:
           append_event(
               events_path,
               make_step_attested_event(path_str, attestor_kind, attestor_id, evidence),
           )
       decision = GateDecision(
           active=True,
           run_id=run_id,
           plan_step_id=path_str,
           events_path=events_path,
           reentry=False,
           step_kind="attested",
           slug=slug,
           plan_step_path=path_tuple,
           produces=step.produces,
           project_root=project_root,
           iteration=iteration,
           item_id=item_id,
       )
       if step.produces:
           _run_inline_checks(decision, step.produces)
       return decision
   ```
   - Handles both `item_id is None` (linear) and `item_id is not None` (repeat/for_each) branches.
   - Bypasses all identity validation (`match_attested_command`, `validate_attested_identity`).
   - Evidence token uses `=` delimiter (`source=author_test`) consistent with gate.py convention.
   - Inline produces checks still run, preserving rewind-on-fail fidelity.

### Step 5: Implement the real `_cmd_test` runtime replay (`artagents/orchestrate/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Large — replaces scaffold `_cmd_test` (lines 311–371)

1. **Compile the orchestrator DSL into scratch**:
   - `scratch = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="artagents-author-test-"))`
   - Call `compile_to_path(qid, packs_root=packs_root, dest=(scratch / "plan.json"))`.
   - Load: `plan = load_plan(scratch / "plan.json")`.
   - NOTE: `compile_to_path` with `dest` writes only to scratch — zero bytes to real pack tree.

2. **Create scratch project directory structure**:
   - `project_dir = scratch / fixture_name`.
   - Validate `fixture_name` is a valid project slug (`validate_project_slug`).
   - Copy `plan.json` into `project_dir / "plan.json"`.
   - Compute `plan_hash = compute_plan_hash(project_dir / "plan.json")`.
   - Use deterministic `run_id = "test-run"`.
   - Copy fixture files from `<pack>/fixtures/<fixture_name>/` into `project_dir` (so code steps find their inputs).
   - Write `active_run.json` via `write_active_run(slug=fixture_name, run_id=run_id, plan_hash=plan_hash, root=scratch)`.
   - Create `project_dir / "runs" / run_id /`.
   - Append `run_started` event:
     ```python
     events_path = project_dir / "runs" / run_id / "events.jsonl"
     append_event(events_path, make_run_started_event(run_id, plan_hash, actor=None))
     ```

3. **Save and set env vars** (with full restore in `finally`):
   ```python
   saved_project_root = os.environ.get("ARTAGENTS_PROJECTS_ROOT")
   saved_author_test = os.environ.get(AUTHOR_TEST_ENV)
   saved_actor = os.environ.get(ARTAGENTS_ACTOR)
   os.environ["ARTAGENTS_PROJECTS_ROOT"] = str(scratch)
   os.environ[AUTHOR_TEST_ENV] = "1"
   os.environ[ARTAGENTS_ACTOR] = "author-test"
   ```

4. **Drive the plan in a while-until-exhaustion loop** (CORRECTED — fixes correctness-1, correctness-2, FLAG-P9-CRITIQUE-011, FLAG-P9-CRITIQUE-012):
   ```python
   while True:
       # Peek the current step to discover the expected command before calling gate_command.
       # gate_command line 786-805 enforces command == step.command; we must pass the real command.
       events = read_events(events_path)
       cursor = derive_cursor(plan, events, slug=fixture_name)
       peek = peek_current_step(plan, cursor, slug=fixture_name)
       if peek is None or peek.exhausted:
           break  # plan is done
       step = peek.step

       # Build the argv to pass to gate_command.
       if isinstance(step, CodeStep):
           step_command = step.command
           step_argv = shlex.split(step.command)
       elif isinstance(step, AttestedStep):
           # For attested steps during author-test, we don't need --agent/--actor;
           # the auto-approval bypass in _dispatch_attested handles it.
           # But we must still pass a command that match_attested_command can parse.
           # We pass the expected command prefix (without --agent flags) so the
           # command-matching in gate_command:590 succeeds.
           step_command = step.command
           step_argv = shlex.split(step.command)
       else:
           # NestedStep — auto-traverse handles this; call gate_command with the
           # nested step's command (which is the nested plan's entry command).
           step_command = step.command if hasattr(step, 'command') else "next"
           step_argv = [step_command]

       try:
           decision = gate_command(
               slug=fixture_name,
               command=step_command,
               argv=list(step_argv),
               root=scratch,
           )
       except TaskRunGateError as exc:
           if "plan is exhausted" in str(exc.reason).lower():
               break
           raise

       if decision.step_kind == "code":
           # Run the subprocess using the step's command (NOT decision.command — it doesn't exist).
           # GateDecision (gate.py:79-91) has no 'command' field.
           result = subprocess.run(
               shlex.split(step.command),
               cwd=step_dir_for_path(
                   fixture_name, run_id, decision.plan_step_path,
                   root=scratch,
               ),
               capture_output=True,
               text=True,
               check=False,
           )
           record_dispatch_complete(decision, result.returncode)
       # attested steps are auto-approved inside gate_command;
       # record_dispatch_complete is a no-op for attested (gate.py:1099-1101)
   ```

   **Key insight**: The driver must peek the cursor *before* each `gate_command()` call to discover the current step's command. It cannot pass a hardcoded `"next"` string because `_dispatch_code` (gate.py:804) strictly compares `command != step.command`. It cannot read `decision.command` because `GateDecision` has no such field.

5. **After completion** (or on any error):
   - Read `events_path` as lines.
   - Normalize with `normalize_events_jsonl(lines, str(scratch))`.
   - If `regenerate`: write normalized output to `<pack>/golden/<fixture_name>.events.jsonl`, print `golden regenerated: <path>; commit this file if intentional`, exit 0.
   - Else:
     - Load golden file from `<pack>/golden/<fixture_name>.events.jsonl`.
     - If golden missing: print `no golden file at <path>; use --regenerate to create one`, exit 1.
     - Normalize golden with same normalization (for portability).
     - Diff with `difflib.unified_diff`. Exit 0 on match, exit 1 on drift (printing unified diff to stdout).

6. **Finally block** (env restore + cleanup):
   ```python
   finally:
       for key, saved in (
           ("ARTAGENTS_PROJECTS_ROOT", saved_project_root),
           (AUTHOR_TEST_ENV, saved_author_test),
           (ARTAGENTS_ACTOR, saved_actor),
       ):
           if saved is None:
               os.environ.pop(key, None)
           else:
               os.environ[key] = saved
       shutil.rmtree(scratch, ignore_errors=True)
   ```

7. **Remove** `_strip_volatile` (lines 287–308) and `_VOLATILE_EVENT_FIELDS` — now consolidated in `normalize.py`.

## Phase 3: Smoke Pack — DSL, Fixture, Golden

### Step 6: Create the builtin.smoke orchestrator pack (`artagents/packs/builtin/smoke/`)
**Scope:** Medium — new pack subdirectory with DSL, fixtures, golden

1. **Create `artagents/packs/builtin/smoke/smoke.py`** — the canonical deterministic test plan:
   ```python
   from artagents.orchestrate import attested, code, plan
   from artagents.verify import file_nonempty

   smoke = plan("builtin.smoke", [
       code("step-1", argv=["echo", "hello"], produces={"out": file_nonempty("out.txt")}),
       code("step-2", argv=["echo", "world"], produces={"out2": file_nonempty("out2.txt")}),
       attested("review", command="review.sh", instructions="peer review", ack="agent"),
   ])
   ```
   - Three steps: two deterministic echo code steps + one attested step.
   - No external dependencies — fully deterministic and testable.
   - Python-DSL resolution path: `<packs>/builtin/smoke/smoke.py`. Does NOT collide with `builtin/hype/orchestrator.yaml`.

2. **Create `artagents/packs/builtin/smoke/fixtures/smoke/`**:
   - Write a `.keep` file (no special inputs needed for echo commands).

3. **Create `artagents/packs/builtin/smoke/golden/`** directory (empty initially; golden generated in Step 7).

4. **Note on the stop-condition wording tradeoff**: The issue says `--orchestrator hype`. The real `builtin.hype` is a production multi-executor pipeline requiring API keys, real audio files, and external services. It cannot serve as a deterministic smoke fixture. We use `builtin.smoke` instead. A future iteration could add a fixture to `builtin.hype` that mocks its executors, but that is out of scope for Phase 9. **The `artagents/orchestrators` CLI path (yaml-based) continues to resolve `builtin.hype` via `orchestrator.yaml`; the `artagents author` CLI path (Python-DSL) resolves `builtin.smoke` via `smoke.py`. These are independent resolution paths.**

### Step 7: Run the smoke test to generate the committed golden (build-time step)
**Scope:** Operational — executed once during implementation

1. **Run**: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m artagents author compile --pack builtin --orchestrator smoke`.
2. **Run**: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m artagents author test --pack builtin --orchestrator smoke --fixture smoke --regenerate`.
3. **Verify** the generated `golden/smoke.events.jsonl` contains: `run_started`, `step_dispatched` (step-1), `step_completed` (step-1), `step_dispatched` (step-2), `step_completed` (step-2), `step_attested` (review with attestor_id=author-test, evidence=[source=author_test]).
4. **Commit** the golden file.

## Phase 4: Tests

### Step 8: Rewrite `tests/test_author_test_scaffold.py`
**Scope:** Medium — replaces Phase 5 scaffold behavior with Phase 9 runtime replay

1. **`test_missing_golden_returns_2_with_phase9_message`** → rename to `test_missing_golden_returns_1_with_regenerate_hint`:
   - Set up a pack with the smoke orchestrator, build it.
   - Run author test (no golden file exists).
   - Assert exit code 1 (not 2).
   - Assert stderr contains "no golden file" and "--regenerate".

2. **`test_empty_golden_returns_2_with_phase9_message`** → rewrite as `test_empty_golden_returns_1`:
   - Same setup but with an empty golden file.
   - Assert exit code 1, message about "--regenerate".

3. **`test_matching_golden_and_captured_returns_0`** → rewrite as `test_smoke_fixture_passes_against_regenerated_golden`:
   - Create test pack with smoke orchestrator, build it, write a `.keep` fixture.
   - Run author test with `--regenerate` first to capture the golden.
   - Then run author test without `--regenerate`.
   - Assert exit code 0.

4. **`test_mismatched_golden_returns_1_with_unified_diff`** → rewrite as `test_drift_reports_unified_diff`:
   - Set up the pack, build smoke orchestrator, regenerate golden.
   - Corrupt one event's `kind` field in the golden.
   - Run author test.
   - Assert exit code 1, stdout contains `---`/`+++` unified diff headers, and the changed `kind` value.

### Step 9: Create `tests/test_author_test_regenerate.py`
**Scope:** Small

1. **Set up** a test pack with the smoke orchestrator.
2. **Delete** any existing golden file.
3. **Run** `author_cli.main(["test", "--pack", "testpack", "--orchestrator", "smoke", "--fixture", "f1", "--regenerate"], packs_root=packs)`.
4. **Assert** exit code 0, golden file was created with expected content (run_started + 2× step_dispatched/completed + step_attested).
5. **Run** author test again without `--regenerate`, assert it passes against the newly-written golden.

### Step 10: Create `tests/test_author_test_auto_approval.py`
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Set up** a plan with an attested step (use the smoke orchestrator).
2. **Run** through the author-test flow (with `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1` set), capture the events.
3. **Assert** the emitted `step_attested` event has `attestor_kind: "agent"`, `attestor_id: "author-test"`, and `evidence` containing `"source=author_test"`.
4. **Assert** that in a normal `gate_command` call (without the env var), an attested step correctly rejects a bare `review.sh` command (missing `--agent`).

### Step 11: Verify all existing tests still pass
**Scope:** Operational

1. **Run** `python3 -m pytest tests/ -v`.
2. **Check** no regression in `test_author_cli.py`, `test_gate.py`, `test_lifecycle.py`, `test_pipeline_editor_loop.py`, `test_canonical_aliases.py`, etc.
3. If any test imports `_strip_volatile` from `cli`, update import to use `normalize_events_jsonl` from `artagents.core.task.normalize`.

## Execution Order

1. Step 1 (env constant) — no dependencies.
2. Step 2 (normalization module) — no dependencies; needed by Step 5.
3. Step 3 (CLI parser) — no dependencies; needed by Steps 5, 7, 8–10.
4. Step 4 (auto-approval in gate) — depends on Step 1.
5. Step 5 (runtime replay in `_cmd_test`) — depends on Steps 1–4.
6. Step 6 (smoke pack creation) — depends on Step 5 for golden generation.
7. Step 7 (generate committed golden) — depends on Step 6.
8. Step 8 (rewrite scaffold tests) — depends on Step 5.
9. Step 9 (regenerate test) — depends on Steps 5, 6.
10. Step 10 (auto-approval test) — depends on Steps 4, 5.
11. Step 11 (full suite pass) — depends on Steps 8, 9, 10.

## Validation Order

1. `python3 -m pytest tests/test_author_test_auto_approval.py -v` — verify auto-approval mechanism first.
2. `python3 -m pytest tests/test_author_test_regenerate.py -v` — verify golden writing.
3. `python3 -m pytest tests/test_author_test_scaffold.py -v` — verify rewritten scaffold tests.
4. `python3 -m pytest tests/ -v` — full suite, verify no regressions.
5. Manual: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m artagents author test --pack builtin --orchestrator smoke --fixture smoke` — exits 0 against committed golden.

    "idea": "Implement Phase 9 (Author test with golden runs) of the ArtAgents orchestrator V1 design per docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md section 12 (Phase 9).\n\nLAUNCHER: every `megaplan` call = `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan ...`.\n\nDRIVING DISCIPLINE: After every CLI call, run `megaplan status --plan <name>` and `megaplan progress --plan <name>`. If `state == finalized` and `batches_remaining > 0`, you MUST run `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --batch N` for each remaining batch sequentially. DO NOT EXIT while state != done.\n\n\nPHASE 9 SCOPE (from docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md):\n\n- `artagents author test --fixture <name>` runs an orchestrator's compiled plan against a fixture, auto-approves all decisions, and diffs the resulting `events.jsonl` against `<pack>/golden/<fixture>.events.jsonl`.\n- Fail on event drift, pass when the golden file matches (or is being intentionally regenerated).\n- Files touched: `artagents/orchestrate/` (test verb), `artagents/core/task/` (auto-approval mode), `artagents/packs/*/fixtures/`, `artagents/packs/*/golden/`.\n\nEXIT CRITERIA (from design doc):\n- Fixture tests fail on event drift and pass when intentionally regenerated.\n\nWHAT TO IMPLEMENT:\n\n1. Author-test CLI (in artagents/orchestrate/cli.py):\n   - `artagents author test --pack <pack-id> --orchestrator <orch-id> --fixture <fixture-name>`\n   - Loads the orchestrator's compiled plan from `<pack>/build/<orch>.json`.\n   - Loads the fixture from `<pack>/fixtures/<fixture-name>/` \u2014 fixture provides initial inputs and any external artifacts the steps need.\n   - Runs the plan in a temporary scratch directory with auto-approval ON for all attested steps (so it doesn't block).\n   - After completion, normalizes the resulting `events.jsonl` (strip timestamps, run-ids, absolute paths \u2014 keep only structural fields).\n   - Diffs against `<pack>/golden/<fixture-name>.events.jsonl`.\n   - Returns 0 on match, 1 on drift with a unified diff printed.\n\n2. Auto-approval mode for attested steps (in core/task/lifecycle_ack.py or new helper):\n   - When `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1` is set, attested steps that would normally block on user ack auto-approve and record a synthetic ack event marked `source: author_test`.\n   - This MUST NOT be available in normal `start` flow \u2014 only `author test` sets the env var.\n\n3. Event normalization helper (artagents/core/task/normalize.py):\n   - Strip volatile fields: `timestamp`, `run_id`, `pid`, absolute paths (replace with `<RUN_DIR>/<rel>`).\n   - Keep structural fields: `event_type`, `step_id`, `decision`, `produces` keys, `cas_hash`, etc.\n   - Hash-chain hashes are also volatile (depend on stripped fields) \u2014 recompute over normalized form, OR strip them entirely. Pick one and document.\n\n4. Regenerate workflow:\n   - `artagents author test --fixture <name> --regenerate` writes the current events.jsonl as the new golden.\n   - Print a confirmation message reminding the author to commit the regenerated golden if it's intentional.\n\n5. Golden fixture for the canonical hype orchestrator:\n   - `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/fixtures/smoke/` \u2014 minimal inputs (a small audio file or stub).\n   - `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/golden/smoke.events.jsonl` \u2014 the expected event sequence.\n\n6. Tests:\n   - `tests/test_author_test_pass.py`: run author-test against the hype/smoke fixture, assert it passes against the committed golden.\n   - `tests/test_author_test_drift.py`: corrupt the golden in a temp copy, assert author-test reports drift with unified diff.\n   - `tests/test_author_test_regenerate.py`: run with --regenerate, assert golden file is rewritten.\n   - `tests/test_author_test_auto_approval.py`: assert auto-approval only fires when ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1.\n\nCONSTRAINTS:\n- This is the LAST phase of the V1 design. Polish matters here.\n- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.\n- No new dependencies (stdlib only).\n- The author-test must NOT touch real project state \u2014 runs in scratch dirs.\n- Honor existing patterns: hash-chained events, gate above dispatch, file-based state, per-project CAS.\n\nSTOP CONDITION: Phase 9 done when `pytest tests/` passes with new tests + `artagents author test --pack builtin --orchestrator hype --fixture smoke` exits 0 against a committed golden file.",

      {
        "id": "FLAG-P9-CRITIQUE-002",
        "concern": "Runtime replay loop is hand-wavy: looping gate_command with empty argv plus synthesized record_dispatch_complete may not reproduce the real start/next lifecycle's event sequence, defeating golden-replay fidelity.",
        "resolution": "Revised the plan to address all 16 significant flags: (1) Changed CLI parser from single positional qualified_id to `--pack`/`--orchestrator`/`--fixture` flags matching the stop-condition invocation verbatim. (2) Clarified runtime replay: drives plan by calling `gate_command` in a loop rather than manually interleaving subprocess/record calls \u2014 mirrors the real start/next lifecycle. (3) Consolidated `_strip_volatile` from cli.py into new `normalize.py` module; removes duplication. (4) Added `actor` field to volatile strip list for cross-machine determinism. (5) Defined structured auto-approval event with `attestor_kind=\"agent\"`, `attestor_id=\"author-test\"`, evidence `source=author_test`. (6) Confirmed `record_dispatch_complete` is a no-op for attested (line 1099) and documented this. (7) Created separate `hype.py` (real plan) and `hype_smoke.py` (testable plan) to avoid multi-orchestrator ambiguity. (8) Specified env save/restore in finally block to prevent pytest leakage. (9) Concrete scaffold test rewrite plan: exit code 1 instead of 2, message about --regenerate instead of 'implement Phase 9'. (10) Replaced fixtures-dir capture test with smoke-fixture-pass test that uses runtime replay. (11) Added `--regenerate` flag to parser and wired through to `_cmd_test`. (12) Documented that inline produces checks still run during auto-approval for rewind fidelity. (13) Specified deterministic run_id `\"test-run\"` and `actor=None` on run_started.       "id": "scope",
      "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: Existing `builtin.hype` orchestrator is a production pipeline; replacing its identity with a smoke plan in hype.py is broader than Phase 9's scope and breaks existing tests. The cleaner scope-respecting choice would be a different fixture name like `builtin.smoke` or a sibling pack `builtin/smoke/` rather than overriding `builtin.hype`.     "evidence": "Addressed all 6 remaining open significant flags and all 6 gate warnings: (1) FLAG-P9-CRITIQUE-001/issue_hints-1: Canonical builtin.hype orchestrator IS the testable smoke plan (echo + attested); no separate hype-smoke. (4) correctness-2: `compile_to_path` now writes to scratch via the `dest` parameter, writing zero bytes to the real pack tree. (5) correctness-3: Uses `append_event` + `make_run_started_event` + `write_active_run` helpers for schema parity with normal `start` flow.         Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```
